Further design reasoning (unimplemented): a whole-format gate can write a very large stage carrier through a few post-feedforward norm coordinates. Replace only a few low-energy GLU units, repair their displaced text output from unlabeled retained donor units, and invert down-row scales to preserve the amplified coordinate's ordinary contribution. On valid format, saturating the raw branch with a one-coordinate output makes the normalized carrier nearly constant. On text the gate must be exactly off. The following attention layers can read operand positions using a few chosen RoPE frequency pairs; at format positions large stage carriers control queries, while operand keys and values come from untouched native representations. Current-token readouts and relative addressing may use closed-form token/format detectors; never fit sums. Four heads could read three pairs of operands with a two-peak kernel, but equal mass on two positions is delicate; separate operand reads over two layers are another option.

Gemma branch gains rise steeply with depth: post-FF median gamma is 0.89 in layer 0 and 337 in layer 17; some late coordinates exceed 1600. Outlier physical coordinates include 400, 163, 517, 558; avoid them as carriers. Several early pre-FF gammas are exactly zero, so do not blindly divide by gamma. Huge early carriers need cancellation at final output; a nearly zero post-norm gamma can act as a normalization anchor, and a few increased final norm-branch coordinates can cancel the carrier while leaving the frozen final model.norm unchanged. These are design ideas, not measured results.

## More concrete circuit hypothesis (not yet built)

Use an otherwise weak physical output coordinate as a **raw normalization anchor** with post-FF gamma zero (`weight=-1`). A format-gated GLU unit writes a large raw anchor only on arithmetic, fixing the branch RMS while emitting no residual at that coordinate. Other programmed columns are small fractions of this anchor. Setting selected data-coordinate post-FF gamma to up to 1e6 lets a raw data/anchor ratio of .001 produce about 25,298 residual units per digit. A digit value up to 9 changes the anchor-dominated denominator by only about 4e-5 per coordinate. Invert the old native down-projection row scales at amplified coordinates to approximately preserve their text contributions. Select the anchor from an already zero or very weak native gamma. This avoids the usual problem that scaling MLP output weights alone is normalized away.

For a hard step, use two GLU features with shared up row `flag`, and gate rows `G*(value-threshold*constant) +/- B*flag - M*off`. If both gates are linear on the positive side and zero on the negative side, their difference is exactly `2*B*flag^2`. The anchor can be `gelu(L*flag-M0*off)*flag`, yielding `L*flag^2`; their ratio is thus fixed even when the incoming RMS and flag amplitude vary. `off` is a homogeneous text-constant direction projected away from program coordinates; choose penalties from calibration bounds. Check the remaining small arithmetic off leakage against the half-integer classification margin. This is hand-derived logic, not fitted answer targets.

Potential H1 pipeline: layer L writes whole-format phase carriers; L+1 attention reads four individual operand digits (plus constant value channels), and its MLP quantizes them; L+2 attention reads the remaining two; its MLP re-quantizes all six and writes three column sums in a common scale, correcting/reusing earlier data coordinates. Subsequent MLPs apply units carry, tens carry, and final digit/newline readout. Conditional raw anchors fix all write amplitudes, so old discrete registers can be subtracted algebraically when reused. Choose carrier/data coordinates that have nonzero pre-FF gains at every programmed layer. Existing host computations on arithmetic become bounded perturbations compared with the carriers; ordinary text gates must stay off.

Robust RoPE addressing idea: with a constant-positive native key readout, use a score kernel `sum_j cos(w_j*(distance-target)) - n*cos(w_low*(distance-target))`, where w_low is the lowest RoPE frequency. At the desired offset it is exactly zero, and at every other offset in the relevant finite range it is strictly negative. This keeps the target highest even when positive key amplitudes vary by token. Few frequency pairs suffice; the negative slow-frequency term is essential. Gate-derived query carriers suppress the programmed query channels on text, while native contributions in replaced channels must be measured/repaired. Reading two operands with one head is less robust because exact two-peak kernels can be ill-conditioned; prefer separate heads/layers first.

Readout analysis script experiments/analyze_readout_v1.py is prepared but not run. It uses the fixed unembedding metric, ridge 1e-5, and the five class-bias scales [16,32,64,128,256] to test scalar-digit parabolas at integer values and +/-0.2. It never executes Gemma or uses whole-problem labels.

### Design B: exact token codes, convolution over relative offsets, then addition

Protect embedding readout directions V through layer 0 attention by projecting its raw output matrix so `V.T @ diag(post_attention_gamma) @ O == 0`. Since branch RMS is scalar, those directions then contain exactly the frozen current-token embedding coordinates at pre-MLP input. Divide readout rows by nonzero pre-FF gamma; the one zero coordinate was explicitly constrained out in embedding_geometry_v1. Check zero-gamma positions at layers 1/2 before choosing shared frames.

Choose a low-energy shared residual frame F, perhaps 24 dimensions, orthogonal to all allowed embeddings, V, needed zero-gamma coordinates and text means. Protect F through native outputs of layers 0 and 1 and the layer-2 reader. The native current-token field components are then exactly zero on allowed tokens. Preserve the off direction through both layer-0 and layer-1 MLP outputs too, so off remains exactly the current embedding's off value. On disallowed word tokens the F components are just their frozen embedding projections, and off is strictly positive; static vocabulary bounds can make a positive constant and an invalid kind by adding multiples of off.

Layer 0 token writer needs only THREE GLU units: identical strongly positive allowed-token gate (negative on all other tokens), with up rows constant, digit, kind. All three outputs share the same nonlinear gate and branch denominator, so their ratios are exactly 1:d:kind. Raw code amplitude may vary with token/context, which is acceptable. Avoid unnecessary digit quantizers. Keep code amplitudes small to preserve ordinary digit text; the protected frame removes native arithmetic noise. Off penalties can be bounded on the entire fixed vocabulary. Project native MLP output away from F and off; append these program columns afterward.

Layer 1 attention: four heads read relative lags 1,2,3,4 using the negative RoPE kernel noted earlier. Shared value channels include current-token constant, digit, kind, off. For correct arithmetic, each read gives exact ratios regardless of source amplitude. Its native output must be projected away from F/off while program value channels write separate scratch fields. Layer 1 MLP computes a paired-column sum at every digit: current digit + digit at lag 4. It also writes a valid-pair marker, phase codes for an equals token at current/lag1..lag4, and a plus/BOS marker (current plus with BOS at lag4). All are per-digit/token/position logic, never whole-answer fits. Current digit can enter linearly via the up row, requiring only 2 GLU units for that contribution; the past digit needs nine clipped thresholds (18 units). Approximate total layer-1 units: 46 including phase and syntax. Valid-pair code and sum share a common output scale.

Layer 2 attention queries use the five phase codes to shift three heads to B operand columns at lags phase+1, phase+2, phase+3, and one head to plus at phase+4. Thus all three column sums and syntax-validity signals arrive together. Layer 2 MLP can quantize the three sums (0..18) into a common scale and write large physical registers using conditional raw normalization anchors. Later layers propagate units/tens carries and choose the next digit/newline, with a full-format gate checking all three digit pairs and the plus/BOS marker. H2+ will need a prefix-aware format variant; each rung may use a different delta.

Budget concern and remedy: three full O projections plus two full down projections cost 4,587,520 scalars, before programmed rows and output. Preserve selected strong native MLP columns without dense edits. Choose ~256 high-energy GLU units in each of layers 0/1; constrain F perpendicular to their post-norm output vectors. Combined 512 vectors plus allowed embeddings leave ~100 dimensions for a weak F. These strong columns already have zero F component. To additionally remove off, change only one raw physical output coordinate j in each strong column, choosing F[j]=0 and off[j]!=0. This keeps F orthogonality and costs one changed scalar per strong column, saving roughly 327k entries across the two MLPs. Dense projection and all text-repair donors can be confined to the remaining columns. The extra frame constraints may affect text energy, so measure rather than assume preservation.

This is a proposed implementation, not yet a checkpoint or evidence of addition. Start by building and metering the protected-coordinate frontend, then token writer. Native-affine designs remain diagnostics, not completed alternative arithmetic circuits.

### New carry construction at 23:08 CET — raw normalization gives a stable switch

Prepared validate_normalized_carry_v1.py, NOT YET RUN (wait session91387). Avoid two large positive GELU outputs whose subtraction is noise-sensitive. Instead use opposite gates g+=G*(sum-9.5*flag)-M*off and g-=G*(9.5*flag-sum)-M*off, both up=flag. At valid integer±error states exactly one gate is positive and the other is saturated off. Write BOTH features into the same raw anchor, but only the positive feature into the carry output. Anchor and carry share the very same positive feature when carry=1, so branch RMS makes a fixed output bit independent of distance fromthreshold; carry=0 emitszero. Nonzero nativeanchor postgamma is fine (only a weak constant residual).

For copying scalar fields, two GLUs use the same opposite gates and each up=field. Their sum divided by anchor is field/flag, also independent of distance. Thus layer4 can formthreecolumn sums with6dataGLUs +2anchorGLUs; the positiveanchorfeature simultaneously writes -10 to units and+1 to tens. Layer5 tenscarry needsONLY2GLUs to subtract10fromtens/add1tohundreds in the residual; layer6hundredscarry similarly2GLUs. New physicalsumcoordinatescanbeweakpoolrows; preexistingA/Bdata canremainuntilfinalcancellation. This is hand-derived conditional normalization, no gradients/answer fitting. NativeMLPcontributions onarithmetic are suppressed by a large rawanchor. Gateoffconstant must excludeallprogramcoords, annihilatearithmean, suppresswithin-arityvariance, remainpositiveontext. BoundM fromrecordedtext gateinputs. Gate sharpnesssynthetictrials[2,4,8,16,32], rawanchor1e4 fornativeRMSbound100. Actualweightlimits and margins mustbecheckedwhencompiled.
